The mark 𝑚 is passive if no guarding role of a schema of 𝐶Ôrequires it and no schema of 𝐶 deletes
it. An agent variable 𝜋 can act is the safe formula 𝑓 (𝜋) ∨ 𝑚 (𝑚(𝜋) ∧ ∃𝑥, 𝑧¯ 𝑢𝑚 (𝜋, 𝑥, 𝑧)),
                                                                                       ¯ over the
marks 𝑚 of 𝑀 that are not passive, 𝑢𝑚 the 𝑢 of the guardianship of 𝑚. 𝐶 has guardianships if every
mark in 𝑀 has one and every guarding role 𝜋 of every schema of 𝐶 forbids 𝜄, requires 𝑓 , requires a
mark in 𝑀 and an atom of the 𝑢 of its guardianship, or has in the body of its schema that 𝜋 can act.
   The ward 𝑥 holds 𝑚 and 𝑢 (𝑏) of every guardian 𝑏, and 𝑏 holds 𝑣 (𝑥) (GnW:1 and GnW:2); the
definition asks nothing of how a ward is created but that her mark come with her identity, so a
ward created with no guardian has left from the start. The last condition says who takes an act:
an agent establishing an identity, a person, or a ward with a guardian, or, where the act is for any
of them, an agent who can act, as any agent who can act may sign an item and forward it [80];
so a ward takes no act without a guardian. A ward is founded by its first guardian, who makes
the ward’s founding will on behalf of the ward: the act that creates a ward is guarded by both,
the guardian having chosen the ward’s key pair, since nothing in the guardian’s state names the
ward before it (Definition 13). A ward of a passive kind wills nothing after its founding, every act
of it being taken for it by its guardians, as the officers of an organisation do. In GnW 𝑣 and 𝑢 are
guardian_of and guarded_by: enter adds ward, identity and guarded_by(Alice) at Carol, who had
no identity, and guardian_of (Carol) at Alice, a person, and make_guardian adds the two records
for another guardian; creator and created record the creation and are no part of the guardianship.
The values 𝑧¯ are for a guardian’s office, as an officer of an organisation holds a role in it, and where
a kind is passive a guardian may be given to a ward that has none, as its bylaws name who appoints
an officer of an organisation that has none.
Grassroots Contracts                                                                                   37


   Definition 33 (Unobstructed among the free). Let 𝐶 have kinds (𝜄, 𝑓 , 𝑀). An introductory act 𝜏 of
𝐶 that adds one atom at each role, of a predicate 𝑒 of arity one, is unobstructed among the free if 𝜏
forbids nothing and every atom 𝜏 requires at a role 𝜋𝑖 is 𝜄; or 𝑓 ; or an atom of a reflexive predicate
naming 𝜋𝑖 ; or, up to renaming the role, the one atom added by some schema of 𝐶 of arity one that
is guarded in its role, has no name term, has literals for its body and head, requires nothing but 𝜄
and 𝑓 , clears nothing and forbids nothing but that atom.
   The act neither requires nor forbids an atom of 𝑒, so two persons may take it whether or not each
holds 𝑒 of the other, and a local state, a multiset, counts the copies; an agent may hold 𝑒 of an agent
they never dealt with, as GKK:3 introduces one person to another. GnW’s acquaint, which requires
identity and person at each role and adds knows of the other there, is unobstructed among the free.
   Definition 34 (Relay act). Let 𝐶 have kinds (𝜄, 𝑓 , 𝑀) and guardianships, and let 𝜏 be an introductory
act of 𝐶 unobstructed among the free, adding atoms of 𝑒. A schema 𝜒 of 𝐶 with no name term and
with literals for its body and head, which forbids nothing, is a relay act with related roles 𝜌 1 ≠ 𝜌 2 ,
roles of it guarded in 𝜒 at each of which 𝜒 adds an atom of 𝑒 naming the other, if:
(1) each related role 𝜌 is a ward role of some 𝑚 ∈ 𝑀, requiring 𝑚, with at most one other role 𝜎 of
    𝜒, its guardian role, requiring an atom of 𝑣 naming 𝜌, the 𝑣 of the guardianship of 𝑚, a guardian
    of the ward, or a free role, requiring 𝑓 ; every role of 𝜒 is a related role or a guardian role; and 𝜒
    adds and deletes at a guardian role no atom naming another guardian role;
(2) every atom 𝜒 requires at a role is 𝜄; or 𝑓 , at a free role or a guardian role; or an atom of a
    reflexive predicate naming the role; or, at a free role or a guardian role, up to renaming the
    role, the one atom of a schema of arity one as in Definition 33; or, at a ward role of 𝑚, its 𝑚, or
    one atom of the 𝑢 of its guardianship, naming its guardian role or, where it has none, an agent
    variable of 𝜒 that occurs in no other atom of 𝜒, the value arguments of the atom variables of 𝜒
    that occur in no other atom 𝜒 requires but the guardian role’s atom of 𝑣; or, at a ward role of 𝑚,
    an atom of a predicate ℎ whose arguments are distinct speech-act variables of 𝜒 occurring in
    no other atom 𝜒 requires but its record below, where every schema of 𝐶 that adds 𝑚 at a role 𝜌
    adds there an atom of ℎ whose arguments are speech-act variables or signed terms, and adds
    its record, an atom of a predicate ℎ ′ whose arguments are 𝜌 and the arguments of that ℎ-atom,
    at the role at which it adds an atom of 𝑣 naming 𝜌; every other schema of 𝐶 that adds an atom
    of 𝑣 naming 𝜌 at a role requires a record ℎ ′ (𝜌, . . .) at a role at which it requires an atom of 𝑣
    naming 𝜌, and adds that record where it adds the atom of 𝑣; and no schema of 𝐶 deletes an
    atom of ℎ or of ℎ ′ ; or, at the guardian role 𝜎 of a ward role 𝜌, its atom of 𝑣 naming 𝜌, or the
    record, the atom of ℎ ′ whose arguments are 𝜌 and the arguments of the ℎ-atom 𝜌 requires; or,
    at a guardian role or a free role, 𝑒 (𝜅) for another guardian role or free role 𝜅, with 𝑒 reflexive
    where both are guardian roles; or, where 𝜒 has two ward roles, at the guardian role of one, 𝑒 of
    the other ward role and its record; and 𝜒 adds at a free role the record of each ward role.
   A relay act is an act by which a ward and another agent come to know each other, with the
guardian of a ward among them where the contract confines the ward to the agents her guardian
knows. GnW’s ward_acquaint is a relay act with a ward role, which requires ward and guarded_by
of a guardian, and a free role, the person, and no guardian role, and wards_acquaint one with
two ward roles and no guardian role. The predicate ℎ of clause 2 is for what a ward carries and
presents to each agent she comes to know, who records it: an AI agent carries the attestation its
principal gave it on entering [80]; GnW has none. In the child-safe social network a child and
an adult come to know each other by a relay act with the child’s parent as the guardian role, a
restriction of ward_acquaint by (d) of Proposition 45, requiring of the parent knows of the adult,
and two children by one with two ward roles and their two parents, requiring of each parent knows
38                                            James Golike, Andy Lewis-Pye, Ehud Shapiro, and Nimrod Talmon


of the other, which binds both parents to one person where the two children share one, knows
being reflexive [79]. Befriending, which requires knows of the other at each role, is no relay act.
  Definition 35 (Introduction with wards). 𝐶 has an introduction with wards if it has kinds (𝜄, 𝑓 , 𝑀)
and guardianships, an introductory act unobstructed among the free, and, for every 𝑚, 𝑚 ′ ∈ 𝑀 that
are not passive, a relay act with a ward role of 𝑚 and a free role and a relay act with a ward role of
𝑚 and a ward role of 𝑚 ′ .

   Theorem 36 (Grassroots realisation with wards). The protocol realising a contract with an
introduction with wards, traceable provenance and volition is volitionally grassroots.
  The proof follows that of Theorem 15. Closure and containment are as there, their proofs using
nothing of the introductory act. Openness is among the agents that have not left: two free agents
come to know each other by the introductory act, and a ward who has not left, and so has a guardian,
and another agent by a relay act, what it requires of their guardians being supplied first by acts
among free agents. A ward with no guardian has left, and so has a ward of a passive kind.

8.1     Guardians and Wards Certified
GnW has an introduction with wards. Its kinds are identity, person and 𝑀 = {ward}: no schema
deletes any of the three; establish, the establishing act, adds identity with person and enter adds
it at Carol with ward, each forbidding it there, and no other schema adds one of them. The
guardianship of ward has guardian_of for 𝑣 and guarded_by for 𝑢. Only retire clears, friend and
guarded_by at Carol, each with a wildcard, deleting guardian_of (Carol) at every Erin she holds
guarded_by of. Enter adds ward at Carol with guarded_by(Alice), Alice a role of it that requires
person and gains guardian_of (Carol). Make_guardian, the other schema adding the two records of
a guardian, adds guarded_by(Bob) at Carol, who requires ward and guarded_by(Alice) and forbids
guarded_by(Bob), and guardian_of (Carol) at Bob, who requires person; end_guardian, resign and
guardian_unfriend delete the two together. Every guarding role forbids identity, as establish’s and
Carol’s in enter; or requires person, as a person’s and a guardian’s, Erin’s among them; or requires
ward and guarded_by, as a ward’s. Acquaint is an introductory act unobstructed among the free: it
adds knows of the other at each role, requires identity and person, and forbids nothing. The relay
acts are ward_acquaint, with the ward role Carol and the free role Bob, and wards_acquaint, with
the ward roles Carol and Dave: each role is guarded and gains knows of the other, a ward role
requires identity, ward and guarded_by of an agent variable, the free role identity and person, and
they forbid nothing.
   The set of the ten predicates has traceable provenance: every atom a schema adds has for its
arguments roles of the schema. Volition holds: establish has arity one; acquaint, befriend, enter,
ward_acquaint, ward_befriend, wards_acquaint and wards_befriend are guarded in every role; in
introduce Alice requires knows of Bob and of Carol, the reading role, and in unfriend friend (Bob);
in make_guardian Alice requires friend (Bob) and guardian_of (Carol), and in end_guardian
guardian_of (Carol), which Bob requires too, as he does in resign and guardian_unfriend; in
ward_unfriend Alice requires guardian_of (Carol) and Carol friend (Bob), which she requires in
ward_end; in retire Alice requires guardian_of (Carol); and the listed roles of make_guardian,
end_guardian and retire have the base Carol.
     Proposition 37 (GnW is grassroots). The protocol realising GnW is volitionally grassroots.
   So the platform realising GnW is a grassroots platform, in which a ward meets, befriends and
leaves as her guardians will. GnW is parametric (Section 3.5), and every instance of it is certified
with it (Theorem 46, clause 5). A contract built on an instance is certified the same way, with its own
Grassroots Contracts                                                                                39


kinds: accountable AI agents with agent and the attestation an AI agent carries [80], the child-safe
social network with child, a parent a guardian role of its relay acts [79], and an organisation with a
passive kind, its officers its guardians, each with a role.

9   RELATIONS AMONG CONTRACTS
Contracts are written from other contracts: GSG extends GKK, GSN and GnW amend GSG, and the
child-safe social network [79] and the network with AI agents [80] are built on both. This section
defines five relations between contracts: amendment, extension and instantiation, on their schemas,
and implementation and restriction, on their runs; and it states what the check of Section 3.8 gives
for a contract so obtained from one already certified. Throughout, 𝐶 and 𝐶 ′ are contracts over
predicates Σ and Σ′ .
    Definition 38 (Parametric contract, instantiation). A parametric contract is a contract some of
whose predicates and constants are parameters, a predicate declared with its arity and the kind
of each of its arguments, agent, speech act, name or value, and a constant with its kind, value
or utterance, each occurring in any of its schemas. An instantiation of it writes, for some of its
parameters, predicates of the same arity and kinds, or, for a predicate that is not a mark, of the
same kinds with further value arguments, and values and utterances of the same kind, that do not
occur in it, a different one for each; in each schema, the atoms of such predicates over one set of
agent variables share a fresh value variable for each added argument, bound by the quantifier that
binds one of those agent variables. An instantiation may also write names of its own for schemas
of the contract, a different one for each, so that an act bears the name its instance gives it, as a
child’s acquaintance is no ward’s; the name of a schema has no part in what the schema denotes.
The term of a parametric contract names its parameters, and that of an instantiation the contract
it instantiates and what it writes for each parameter and for each schema it names. A parametric
contract has no realisation: Definition 16 is for a contract none of whose predicates or constants is
a parameter, and only an instantiation of every parameter of a parametric contract is realised, as a
protocol and as an app.
   Definition 39 (Amendment, extension, instantiation). (1) 𝐶 ′ amends 𝐶 if it is obtained from 𝐶 by
    deleting schemas and adding schemas.
(2) 𝐶 ′ extends 𝐶 if it amends 𝐶 deleting nothing.
(3) 𝐶 ′ instantiates 𝐶 if 𝐶 is parametric and 𝐶 ′ is obtained from 𝐶 by an instantiation.
   Definition 40 (Lift, extension at an argument). Let 𝐶 be a contract and 𝐹 a set of its predicates.
The lift of 𝐶 at 𝐹 is the contract obtained from 𝐶 by giving each predicate of 𝐹 one more argument,
a value, and, in each schema of 𝐶, adding one value variable, fresh to the schema, as that argument
of every atom of a predicate of 𝐹 . 𝐶 ′ extends 𝐶 at 𝐹 if 𝐶 ′ extends the lift of 𝐶 at 𝐹 .
   Each act of 𝐶 stays, in its lift, within one value of the added argument. A contract of denominated
coins and bonds, in preparation, extends GB [81] at its coin and bond predicates, the denomination
being the added argument; its acts across denominations are among the schemas it adds. GSN
amends GSG, deleting its unfriend and adding its own and the schemas of messages and groups. The
composition of two contracts amending a third is the schemas of the third that both keep together
with the schemas each adds; it amends each, and where both extend the third, as GSN and GC
extend GKK, it is their union, which extends each; one app runs it (Section 1). A contract extends
several contracts when it extends their union, as a contract for grassroots banks, in preparation,
extends the contracts for grassroots organisations and for denominated coins and bonds, and is
checked as an extension of the union, the union an extension of each. GnW is parametric in its
six predicates of wards, ward, creator, created, guardian_of , guarded_by and retired: the child-safe
40                                               James Golike, Andy Lewis-Pye, Ehud Shapiro, and Nimrod Talmon


social network [79] instantiates them at predicates for a child and her parents, accountable AI
agents [80] at predicates for an AI agent and the persons operating it, and a contract for grassroots
organisations, in preparation, at predicates for an organisation and its officers; so each has GnW’s
acts and restates none, a platform runs an instance and none runs GnW, and one with wards of two
kinds has two instances and the acts by which a ward of one comes to know a ward of the other.
GC with its coin predicate as a parameter is a contract for any asset issued by an agent, instantiated
at ¢ by GC itself, and a contract for a bank whose bylaws are a constant parameter is instantiated
by each bank at its own bylaws, as a contract for grassroots organisations, in preparation, does: its
acts ask whether the bylaws admit them, so no bank runs the parametric contract, which has no
bylaws, and each bank runs its own instance; a contract is parametric in the types of its values
without declaration (Appendix C).
   Definition 41 (Implementation, restriction). Let 𝐶 and 𝐶 ′ be contracts with traceable provenance
that satisfy volition, and F and F ′ the protocols realising them (Definition 16). 𝐶 ′ implements 𝐶
through a map 𝜇 from the local states of 𝐶 ′ to those of 𝐶 if for every 𝑃 ⊂ Π and every correct
run 𝑟 ′ of F ′ (𝑃) there is a correct run 𝑟 of F (𝑃) whose machine configurations, with consecutive
repetitions removed, include those of 𝑟 ′ , mapped by 𝜇 and with consecutive repetitions removed,
in order, from the first and, where these are finitely many, to the last. 𝐶 ′ restricts 𝐶 if Σ′ ⊆ Σ and 𝐶 ′
implements 𝐶 through the identity.
   Where those configurations are all of those of 𝑟 , this is the correct implementation of [51], read
on machine configurations, its stutters removed; in addition, a run of 𝐶 ′ may take in one act what
𝐶 takes in several. A restriction allows fewer runs: every correct run of 𝐶 ′ is, on the machines, one
of 𝐶. An implementation may also add records the implemented contract lacks.
   Proposition 42 (Restriction is undecidable). Whether one syntactically grassroots contract
restricts another is undecidable.
  Restriction is therefore approximated by syntactic conditions that imply it. The set of syntactic
approximations is dynamic, and we hope and expect it to grow; the four below are proved in
Appendix B. Each strengthens a body, in the following sense.
   Definition 43 (Stronger body). Let 𝜏 and 𝜏 ′ be schemas with the same head, every role of 𝜏 a role
of 𝜏 ′ and every variable of 𝜏 a role or a variable of 𝜏 ′ . The body of 𝜏 ′ is stronger than that of 𝜏 if 𝜏 ′
requires at each role of 𝜏 every atom 𝜏 requires there and its body holds every safe formula of the
body of 𝜏, the speech-act variables and name terms occurring in the atoms the two require at their
roles are the same, and the body of 𝜏 holds at every machine configuration 𝑑 and binding 𝛽 for 𝜏 ′
at which that of 𝜏 ′ holds, a body holding at 𝑑 and 𝛽 when they meet the conditions Definition 7
sets on its atoms, its safe formulas and its constraint.
   Whether one body is stronger than another is undecidable in general, a body being a formula of
first-order logic with counting; the checker decides it where the stronger body holds every literal
and every safe formula of the weaker and its constraint implies the weaker’s, a sufficient condition.
   Lemma 44 (Stronger body). Let the body of 𝜏 ′ be stronger than that of 𝜏, every role of 𝜏 ′ that is
not one of 𝜏 an agent role, and every binding admissible for 𝜏 ′ admissible for 𝜏. If 𝜏 ′ determines 𝑑 → 𝑑 ′
at an admissible binding 𝛽, then 𝛽 is admissible for 𝜏, 𝜏 determines at 𝛽 the restriction of 𝑑 → 𝑑 ′ to
the agents in the extents in 𝑑 of the roles of 𝜏, and every other agent 𝑝 of 𝑑 has 𝑑𝑝′ = 𝑑𝑝 .
  A binding admissible for 𝜏 ′ is admissible for 𝜏 where every guarding role of 𝜏 ′ is one of 𝜏;
where no speech-act variable of 𝜏 occurs in an atom it adds and in none it requires; and where the
constraint of 𝜏 ′ has every such variable signed by the agent in a guarding role of 𝜏 (Definition 5).
Grassroots Contracts                                                                                      41


   The approximations go class by class, so two of them need the classes of the acts no person wills
to be those of one schema at one binding. A contract has simple unguarded classes if every class
of its realisation that contains a machine transaction underlying an unguarded transaction is the
set of machine transactions one schema with no guarding agent role determines at one admissible
binding, and no other schema, nor that schema at another binding, determines a member of it. A
contract has them where each schema with no guarding agent role has an atom it adds or deletes at
an agent role, the only atom with its predicate that a schema of the contract adds, deletes or clears,
among whose arguments, or in name terms among them, every agent role and every variable of
the schema occurs, as the delivery of a contract for feeds and followers, in preparation, has.
   Proposition 45 (Syntactic restrictions). Let 𝐶 and 𝐶 ′ have traceable provenance and satisfy
volition, with Σ′ ⊆ Σ. 𝐶 ′ restricts 𝐶 if it is obtained from 𝐶 by one of the following, and, restriction
being transitive, by a sequence of them:
(a) replacing a schema 𝜏 with a guarding agent role by a schema 𝜏 ′ with its name and guards and a
    stronger body, every role of 𝜏 ′ that is not one of 𝜏 an agent role that is a reading role, or deleting
    such a schema, 𝐶 and 𝐶 ′ having simple unguarded classes;
(b) adding a schema with no guarding role, with the head and the roles of a schema 𝜏 of 𝐶 and a body
    stronger than that of 𝜏;
(c) replacing a schema 𝜏 with no guarding agent role by a schema 𝜏 ′ with its name, roles and guards
    and a stronger body, where at every configuration of every correct run of the protocol realising
    𝐶 ′ and at every admissible binding the body of 𝜏 ′ holds wherever that of 𝜏 does, 𝐶 and 𝐶 ′ having
    simple unguarded classes;
(d) replacing a schema 𝜏 with a guarding agent role by a schema 𝜏 ′ with its name and a stronger body,
    whose guarding roles are those of 𝜏 and agent roles that are no roles of 𝜏, every other role of 𝜏 ′ that
    is not one of 𝜏 an agent role that is a reading role, and every binding admissible for 𝜏 ′ admissible
    for 𝜏, 𝐶 and 𝐶 ′ having simple unguarded classes.
   Under (a) a person may will fewer acts, and an act may read more agents’ states; under (b)
the machine takes, unasked, an act a person may will, when a stronger body holds; (c) needs an
invariant of the contract, proved for each contract; and under (d) an act takes the will of more
persons, who change no state, as a child comes to know an agent with the consent of a parent [79],
the name of a group the child creates being still the child’s to sign. Deleting a schema with no
guard is not a restriction in general, as the proof of Proposition 42 shows, and nor is strengthening
its body without such an invariant: either may drop an act the original owes. An undertaking
formula (Definition 50) is part of a clause and denotes no transaction, so adding an undertaken
clause with one leaves the schemas and the runs as they are: a contract for distrust among friends,
in preparation, is restricted by strengthening the body of befriending, by (a), and by adding a clause
whose formula says that Alice, whose friend Bob distrusts Carol, will eventually distrust Carol or
end her friendship with Bob.
   The three conditions are modular in the following sense, where the schemas an amendment
keeps are those of 𝐶 it does not delete.
   Theorem 46 (What the check preserves). Let 𝐶 be syntactically grassroots.
(1) If 𝐶 ′ amends 𝐶, then 𝐶 ′ is syntactically grassroots if and only if 𝐶 ′ has an unobstructed introductory
    act, every atom an added schema adds meets Definition 11 in 𝐶 ′ with 𝐸 the predicates of Σ′ other
    than environment predicates, every added schema meets Definition 13 in 𝐶 ′ , and every kept schema
    with no guarding role and a reading role has signature atoms in 𝐶 ′ .
(2) If 𝐶 ′ is obtained from 𝐶 by deleting schemas and by replacing schemas 𝜏 by schemas 𝜏 ′ with the
    name and the head of 𝜏 and a stronger body, the guarding roles of 𝜏 ′ those of 𝜏 and agent roles
42                                              James Golike, Andy Lewis-Pye, Ehud Shapiro, and Nimrod Talmon


    that are no roles of 𝜏, every other role of 𝜏 ′ that is not one of 𝜏 an agent role that is a reading role
    and 𝜏 then having a guarding agent role, then 𝐶 ′ is syntactically grassroots if and only if 𝐶 ′ has
    an unobstructed introductory act and every such added reading role has an edge to a guarding role
    from an atom that guarding role requires.
(3) If 𝐶 ′ instantiates 𝐶, then 𝐶 ′ is syntactically grassroots.
(4) The lift of 𝐶 at any set of its predicates is syntactically grassroots; so 𝐶 ′ extending 𝐶 at it is
    syntactically grassroots if and only if it meets the conditions of clause 1 as an amendment of that
    lift deleting nothing.
(5) Clauses 1 to 3 hold with ‘has an introduction with wards, traceable provenance and volition’
    (Section 8) for ‘is syntactically grassroots’, of 𝐶 and of 𝐶 ′ , and ‘an introduction with wards’ for ‘an
    unobstructed introductory act’.

Implementable contracts. A contract written at the level of its clauses may be beyond what a
compiler realises: vGLP’s compiler [47] realises a schema with no guard only where one machine’s
state names the whole act. A contract is implementable if, in each schema with no guarding role,
the atoms its first role requires name every other role and every variable of the atoms it adds and
deletes; this is decidable on the schemas. The abstraction level of a contract is not restricted by
it: an implementable contract restricts, or implements, one that is not. A contract for feeds and
followers, in preparation, delivers to Alice every item of Carol’s feed that Bob holds, Alice following
Carol’s feed through Bob, by a schema with no guard in which Alice’s state names Bob and the feed
and not the item, and Bob’s names the item and not Alice; its implementable restriction requires
that Bob know everyone who points to him, and so Alice, and restricts it by (a) and then (c), an
invariant of the restriction ensuring that Bob knows Alice.
    The proofs are in Appendix B. Provenance and Volition are conditions on each schema given
the predicates of the contract, and hold of a kept schema in every amendment, but for signature
atoms, which an added unguarded schema may take; Introduction is the one condition on the whole
contract, since a schema added anywhere may obstruct the introductory act. So an owner amending
a certified contract checks the added schemas and the introductory act, an owner strengthening
bodies or adding guards checks the introductory act and any reading role it adds, and a composition
of two certified contracts is checked as an extension of either; the checker does all. The three
conditions read of a predicate only its arity and the kinds of its arguments, so a parametric contract is
checked once for all its instances, and the converse of clause 3 holds too; the conditions of Section 8
read no more of one, so the same holds of a contract with wards. Definition 41 and Proposition 45
ask of a contract traceable provenance and volition, which is what makes its realisation contained
and well defined, and not that it be certified; so a certified contract may restrict one that is not.
Accountable AI agents [80] instantiate GnW, compose the instance with GFP, both amending GSG,
and restrict the composition by (a): in the composition any agent with an identity signs an item
and forwards it, a ward with no guardian among them, so it has no guardianships (Definition 32),
and the restriction adds to the body of each of the two acts that its agent can act. No clause of GFP
or of GnW is amended, and signing is one act with one name for a person and for an AI agent. The
relations are the inheritance, refinement and generics of programming, compared in Section 12.

10   LEGAL MODALITIES OF CONTRACT CLAUSES
The schemas also determine, for each clause, whether an agent can breach it and what record of
it the contract leaves in another agent’s state: its legal modality, its modality for short. The two
contracts show it. A clause may be one no act of the contract can breach, one an agent may breach
with the contract recording the breach, or one whose performance is the person’s and not the
machine’s, of which the contract records the undertaking. The question is about every clause, not
Grassroots Contracts                                                                                   43


only about those that describe no act. The three are what a realisation does with a clause, and are
not kinds of obligation: a friendship kept on paper can be entered against an agent’s will, and under
the schemas of Section 3.4 it cannot.
  Throughout this section 𝐶 is a syntactically grassroots contract. An agent joins the platform
realising 𝐶 by signing an undertaking to keep 𝐶 towards all its other participants, which they
present to every participant they interact with (Section 11).
   Definition 47 (Clause). A clause of a contract 𝐶 is a proposition about the conduct of its agents,
and is kept or breached by that conduct; an agent the clause names as answerable is its obligor. The
runs of the protocol realising 𝐶 determine the clause if whether it is kept is a function of the run. A
transaction of 𝑅𝐶 may breach the clause if it is taken in some correct run and taking it breaches the
clause.
  Definition 48 (Witnessed). A volition-guarded transaction (𝑑 → 𝑑 ′, 𝑄 ′ ) over participants 𝑄 is
witnessed against 𝑎 ∈ Π if 𝑑𝑏′ \ 𝑑𝑏 holds an atom naming 𝑎 for some 𝑏 ∈ 𝑄 with 𝑏 ≠ 𝑎. A schema is
witnessed against an agent role if every transaction it determines is witnessed against the agent in
that role.
   That atom is one the act put in the state of another agent, and by Lemma 27 an edge 𝑏 → 𝑎 of
the recording graph of 𝑑 ′ that 𝑑 did not have. A record serves as evidence when the act put it there,
it names the agent answerable, and someone else holds it.
  Definition 49 (Enforced, Attested, Undertaken). Let 𝜑 be a clause of 𝐶, with obligor 𝑜 where it has
one. Then 𝜑 is
(1) enforced by 𝐶 if the runs of the protocol realising 𝐶 determine it and every correct run of that
    protocol keeps it;
(2) attested by 𝐶 if some transaction of 𝑅𝐶 may breach it and every transaction that may is witnessed
    against 𝑜;
(3) undertaken by 𝐶 if no transaction of 𝑅𝐶 may breach it, so that a breach, where the runs
    determine the clause, is an omission of 𝑜, and some transaction of 𝑅𝐶 is witnessed against 𝑜 or
    𝑜’s undertaking is held by another agent.
   The three are exclusive and not exhaustive. Two kinds of clause have none. One that some
transaction may breach without witnessing against the obligor can be breached invisibly. One that
the runs neither determine nor record is owed by no one identifiable. A realisation does nothing
with either.
   An undertaken clause may say what its obligor undertakes in the contract’s own terms, as a
formula over runs: that whenever a condition on the agents’ states holds, a response eventually
holds, the leads-to of temporal logic [55, 64]. A clause of a restriction of a contract for distrust among
friends, in preparation, reads “Alice, whose friend Bob distrusts Carol, will eventually distrust
Carol or end her friendship with Bob”: its formula is friend (Alice, Bob) ∧ distrusts(Bob, Carol) ∧
¬distrusts(Alice, Carol) { distrusts(Alice, Carol) ∨¬friend (Alice, Bob), and the acts by which Alice
keeps it are the contract’s.
   Definition 50 (Undertaking formula). An undertaking formula of a clause of 𝐶 with obligor 𝑜
is 𝜑 { 𝜓 , with 𝜑 and 𝜓 quantifier-free formulas over atoms whose arguments are the clause’s
names, speech-act variables and name terms, the first argument of each atom its holder, universally
quantified over the names and the variables. A run of the protocol realising 𝐶 keeps it if, at every
binding of the names and the variables, whenever 𝜑 holds at the machines of a configuration of the
run, 𝜓 holds at that configuration or a later one; otherwise 𝑜 breaches the clause in the run.
44                                          James Golike, Andy Lewis-Pye, Ehud Shapiro, and Nimrod Talmon


   A run that does not keep an undertaking formula is correct: the breach is the person’s and no
fault of the machines, and the formula adds nothing to liveness. The runs therefore determine the
clause, no transaction may breach it, and it is undertaken; the theorems of a contract may be stated
for the runs in which every person keeps their undertakings, and for those in which some do not.
A formula says nothing to the three conditions, which do not read it.
   A schema names its agent role 𝜋 𝑗 at another agent role 𝜋𝑖 if an atom added at 𝜋𝑖 names 𝜋 𝑗 .
  Proposition 51 (Witnessing). A schema is witnessed against every agent role it names at another
agent role.
  A predicate 𝑒 is guarded in 𝐶 if whenever a schema of 𝐶 adds an 𝑒-atom, the atom names only
roles of that schema that will the act. The consent clause for 𝑒 says that an agent’s state holds an
𝑒-atom only if those the atom names willed the act that added it.
  Proposition 52 (Enforcement by guarding). If 𝑒 is guarded in 𝐶, the consent clause for 𝑒 is
enforced by 𝐶.

The clauses of the contracts. GKK:2, GKK:3 and GSG:2 are enforced, by the schemas requiring
what the clauses require: a person knows another only by an act of both or through someone
who knows them. GSG:1 is attested. Befriending is an act, and Proposition 51 says it is witnessed
against both persons; so if Alice befriends Bob without trusting him, Bob holds the record of her
breach. No act can breach the rest of the clause: nobody becomes a friend of Alice without her
will (Proposition 52), and nobody befriends a person whose public key they do not hold. GSN:1
to GSN:3 permit acts and oblige nothing, unfriend ending every membership between the two at
once, as GSN:3 says; and the delivery of GSN:4 is unguarded and so taken in every correct run
in which it stays enabled, reaching every current member, a member when the leader’s delivery
to them is taken, since delivery to each member is an act of its own, taken after the message is
posted: the runs determine GSN:1 to GSN:4 and every correct run keeps them, so they are enforced.
GSG:3 is undertaken: ceasing to trust is no act of the contract, so no act can breach the clause and
the runs do not determine it, and Alice’s undertaking of Section 11, in Bob’s hands, is its record.
GFP:1 and GFP:2 are enforced: a person holds an item only as they signed it or as a friend signed it
over to them, and Bob’s item(sgn(𝑥, Alice)) carries every signature. GKK:1 is undertaken: no act
can breach the establishing of an identity, and being accountable for what is signed with a key is
not an act of the contract, so the runs do not determine the clause, and the agent’s undertaking of
Section 11, which another agent holds, is its record.
The clauses of GC. GC:1 to GC:4 permit acts and oblige nothing, and the knowing GC:2 to GC:4
require the schemas require, so the runs determine them and every correct run keeps them: they
are enforced. GC:5 is undertaken, a mutual credit line adding ¢(Bob) at Alice and ¢(Alice) at Bob
and so being witnessed against both of them. The clause that a coin enters a holding only by its
issuer’s will has no modality, being breachable by pay without pay naming the payer.
Performance outside the system. GC:5 and GSG:1 rest on what is done outside the schemas,
since selling at an advertised price is no act of the contract and neither is trusting a person, and
they have different modalities, the first undertaken and the second attested (Section 10). The three
modalities therefore do not divide by whether performance is digital.
Attestation and undertaking. The two require the same thing of the schemas, that the act naming
the obligor leave the record in another agent’s hands, and differ only in whether the breach is
itself an act of the contract. The evidence depends on the schemas and the modality on the clause.
A performance promised for later is never attested, because failing to perform is not an act; a
Grassroots Contracts                                                                                45


warranty given in the course of an act is attested exactly when that act is witnessed against the
agent giving it.
   Witnessing therefore depends on whether an effect names the agent performing the act, and not
only the agents the atom concerns. Unfriend adds no atom, so no clause against ending a friendship
at will can be attested under GSG; pay adds a coin naming its issuer and not its payer, so no clause
against the payer can be attested under GC. A contract whose breaches are to be attested must
have acts whose effects name their author. Signing provides it: an act taken as a digital speech act,
an utterance signed with the key of the agent taking it, leaves an atom naming that agent, and the
signed record is non-repudiable [12, 32].

11   SIGNING THE CONTRACT
A person joins a global platform by signing an End-User Licence Agreement with its operator.
An agent joins a grassroots platform by signing a one-sided undertaking to keep its grassroots
contract towards all its other participants, and presents it to every participant they interact with;
the code realising the contract, or the platform hosting it, enforces this, refusing an interaction
with a participant that has not presented one. The undertaking is a speech act, signed with the
key of the agent giving it and non-repudiable [12, 32]. An agent signs by themselves, before their
first interaction; where a participant invites them, the invitation, signed by the inviter, names the
contract, and the invitee presents their undertaking first to the inviter.
   Presentation is not part of the protocol: the code carries the undertaking in the messages of an
interaction, and it leaves no atom in a state. Throughout this section 𝐶 is a syntactically grassroots
contract, F the protocol realising it and 𝑄 ⊂ Π; write 𝑝 ▷ 𝑞, for 𝑝, 𝑞 ∈ 𝑄, when 𝑝 has presented its
undertaking to 𝑞.
  Definition 53 (Presenting run). A run of F (𝑄) with a relation ▷ on 𝑄 is presenting if 𝑝 ▷ 𝑞 and
𝑞 ▷ 𝑝 for every interaction of the run between 𝑝 and 𝑞.
  The code realising 𝐶, or the platform hosting it, keeps every run presenting.
  Proposition 54 (Every participant has presented its undertaking). In a presenting run,
every agent of an instance of two or more agents has presented their undertaking to another agent of
that instance.
   By Section 10 a record is evidence when it names the agent answerable and is held by someone
else; the undertaking names its signer, and by the proposition another participant holds it. Condition
(5) of Section 1 is therefore enforced by the code.
The code. Enforced clauses are enforced by the code the agents run: a breach of one is impossible
with a correct realisation of the contract (Section 10), so a breach requires an agent to run other
code. The code is warranted by its creator, a natural or legal person, and by its compiler. Each
warranty is signed and carried with the code: the creator’s, that the code realises the contract,
carrying the contract’s schemas and the checker’s verdict that they are syntactically grassroots;
the compiler’s, that the compiled code is the creator’s. Neither need have adopted the contract, so
the warranties are not clauses of the contract and have no modality under Definition 49; a breach
of either is provable from the warranted code and the contract by anyone holding both. In their
undertaking an agent also promises to run the warranted code. This clause is undertaken: no act
of the contract can breach it, no run shows whether an agent keeps it, and the undertaking, in
another agent’s hands, is its record. Where the platform hosting the code attests it, every interaction
opening with proof of the application each agent runs, an application that runs only warranted
code, the clause is enforced by the platform.
46                                             James Golike, Andy Lewis-Pye, Ehud Shapiro, and Nimrod Talmon




Fig. 6. An illustration of GKK as an app of GAS on three phones: Alice has become acquainted with Bob and
with Carol (GKK:2), and Bob and Carol do not know each other. The screens are those of three agents, each
with its own networking layer, run on one machine; the phones are drawn. Before their first message each
agent presented their undertaking to the other.


GAS. GAS, the GLP app store of Section 1, makes an app of every contract the repository records
down to its platform, and does what this section asks of the code. The code it warrants is the
contract’s own: the platform compiled from the contract’s schemas and pragmas, and one person
of it, which GAS’s host code gives the person’s name, network and screen, the day for a contract
with a calendar, and nothing else. The compiler’s warranty is the certificate GLP’s compiler writes
for every program it compiles: the compiler’s key, the identity of the program’s source and that of
its compiled code, and the compiler’s signature over the two. The compiler refuses a certificate to
a program that reaches the network or the person other than through what its host gives it. The
creator’s warranty is the creator’s signature over the identity of the source, a hash of the contract’s
schemas, the checker’s verdict, and a hash of the host code that runs it. A device installs a contract
only after compiling its code itself and checking the identities and signatures of both warranties, so
it installs no contract whose own code is refused a certificate. The host code then loads the code the
device compiled, which GLP’s loader admits only as a program whose certificate checks, and runs
it only where its compiler is the device’s own and the creator’s warranty is for the same source.
    In GAS the undertaking names the contract’s package, and with it the warranted code. An agent
presents it to each agent before its first message to them, and the messages of an agent who has not
presented theirs are held from the contract’s code until they do; each agent keeps the undertakings
presented to it. An invitation is the inviter’s signed statement naming the package; the invitee
checks it, keeps it, and presents their undertaking to the inviter first. On Android and the iPhone,
where the phone’s platform attests GAS itself, the platform attests each connection, naming the
application at its other end, and GAS by default gives the contract’s code the messages of a person
only while a connection from them is attested as GAS; the clause that an agent runs the warranted
code is then enforced, by the platform’s attestation and GAS’s own checks. Figure 6 illustrates GKK
as an app of GAS.
Grassroots Contracts                                                                                 47


Electronic signatures. An undertaking signed with an agent’s key is an electronic signature. In the
United States a signature, contract or record may not be denied legal effect, validity or enforceability
solely because it is in electronic form [92]; in the European Union an electronic signature shall not
be denied legal effect and admissibility as evidence solely on the grounds that it is in electronic
form [21]; and in the United Kingdom an electronic signature, and its certification, are admissible
in evidence on the authenticity and integrity of the data signed [91]. These statutes settle the form;
whether an undertaking is enforceable is a question for the ordinary law of contract: the agents of
a grassroots contract freely accept and sign it, as condition (5) requires, and its realisation keeps
signed records of the acts that follow.

12   RELATED WORK
Each paragraph below is expanded in Appendix D.
Contracts as formal objects. The formal study of contracts in this field is dominated by the
contract-as-automaton family — contract automata [5], a loan agreement as a finite automaton [24],
the L4 language [93], timed contract automata [13], and encodings as defeasible deontic rules [36]
and the comparison of imperative with declarative smart contracts [35]. All fix the parties when
the contract is formed, keep one contract state, and assume an alphabet of events, a clock or a
ledger, where our conditions quantify over the family of systems a contract induces on every
finite set of people, need a local state per agent, and forbid a participant without a role in the act.
Symboleo [63, 85] and Stipula [16] are the nearest to ours and make the same three commitments;
Catala [56] does the corresponding work for statute. Befriending cannot be written in Stipula,
which allows joint consent only in the constructor. We claim no new language: the conditions are
on the four things a schema carries, which Stipula’s functions and Symboleo’s obligations also
carry, given a semantics in which each agent holds its own state. BSPL [87] specifies an interaction
protocol by message schemas between roles, with no global state: the local state of a role is the
history of its messages, and a role may send a message once it knows the information the message
requires, as a speech-act schema requires atoms at a role; a message passes between two roles,
where an act may take any number of roles and the will of each.
Dynamic and temporal logic. A speech-act schema is a program of first-order dynamic logic [42],
bind the variables, test the body and the wills, apply the head, and its denotation is that program’s
relation on states, with agent configurations as the states; a formula [𝜏]𝜑 states what holds after
an act. The body of a schema is a safe query of the relational calculus [15], with the aggregate
of counting [1], and its head an update of the agents’ states. Dynamic logic says nothing of runs:
liveness, that an enabled act is eventually taken, and interactivity, that from every state some
continuation interacts, are formulas of temporal logic [19, 64] over the transition system a contract
denotes, and process logic [41] joins the two. Neither has persons with wills, nor obliviousness,
which relates the systems of two sets of agents to that of their union; both are [51]’s.
Inheritance, refinement and generics. Amendment is the law’s own word, and in programming
extension is subclassing, a class adding methods to its parent’s, and an amendment that rewrites a
method is overriding. Behavioural subtyping [54] and design by contract [57] let a subclass weaken
a method’s precondition and strengthen its postcondition, so that it stands in for the parent, and so
do the refinement calculus [6]; a restriction of Section 9 is CSP’s refinement [44], fewer behaviours,
every correct run of the restriction being, on the machines, one of the original, and its syntactic
approximations strengthen preconditions, which none of them allows. Instantiation is parametric
polymorphism [58], a contract generic in a predicate as a program is in a type, and the types of
Grassroots Logic Programs [84] are the realisation’s. In the specification calculi, including a Z
schema in another [88] is extension and the B method’s refinement [2] weakens preconditions as
48                                            James Golike, Andy Lewis-Pye, Ehud Shapiro, and Nimrod Talmon


the refinement calculus does; in algebraic specification [31] an extension that proves nothing new
of the old symbols is conservative, which is what Theorem 46 shows of the check, and a theory
morphism, renaming symbols with their signatures, is instantiation. Contract law has the words:
an amendment modifies a contract’s terms, a supplement adds to them, and a novation replaces the
contract by another [3, §§89, 280]. None says what a syntactic check preserves; Theorem 46 does.
Norms and normative systems. Contracts are represented as defeasible rules with deontic
operators, against which compliance is checked [34], and normative multi-agent systems concern
how norms are operationalised and brought into conflict [9]; a guard [51] is a condition of enactment
rather than a deontic operator. A social commitment [86] is of a debtor to a creditor in a context
group, whose norms settle disputes between the two; an undertaken clause of Section 10 is a
commitment of its obligor to every other participant, with no group above the agents, and another
agent holds its record.
Juridical acts and declarative power. What we call an act is the result-declaration of Sartor [68],
modelled by Hage [40], and a declarative power exercised jointly is a contract [28, 29]. There a
proclamation has effect only if an institution provides for it [25, 28, 38, 45]; a grassroots contract
has no institution, so an act’s effect is the change in the agents’ own states, and its guard takes the
place of authorisation. An electronic institution [20] specifies the scenes in which agents in roles
exchange speech acts, with institutional roles that external agents cannot play, and the institution
keeps each agent’s obligations; a grassroots contract has no institutional role, and the records of its
acts are in the agents’ own states.
Standard-form contracting. The End-User Licence Agreement is a contract of adhesion, treated
by Radin [66], Kim [48] and Ben-Shahar and Schneider [7]; a grassroots contract has no drafting
party, so the questions of that literature do not arise.
Terms a machine can evaluate. Surden [89], smart legal contracts [14] and enforceable security
policies [69] each divide a contract’s terms in two by what a realisation can do with them; Section 10
divides them in three, and the third class, a breach recorded against its obligor, is the one a remedy
needs. Compliance monitors receive inputs from trusted observers [11, 60]; a monitor is a participant
without a role in the act, and closure forbids one.
Code as a regulator. Lessig [49] argued that architecture regulates as code, and the digital social
contract [12] and smart contracts [17] make the contract a program; a grassroots contract is not
code, its conditions being checked on the text.
Self-governance. Ostrom [62] and commons scholarship [8, 43] concern communities governing a
resource without an owner or a state, and platform regulation [22] binds an operator; a grassroots
contract governs a relationship, and a platform constituted from its members’ own devices [74, 76,
77] has no operator.
Grassroots systems. Grassroots protocols are due to [74] and were recast with volition-guarded
transactions in [51]; platforms have been specified for social networking [75] and for personal
cryptocurrencies [50, 73, 81]. Secure Scuttlebutt [90] is a peer-to-peer social network in which
each person chooses their own key pair, signs each message of their own append-only log, and
replicates the logs of those they follow, with no central authority; following takes no consent of the
one followed, where befriending under GSG:1 takes the will of both. PGP’s Web of Trust [94, 95]
has every person as an introducer vouching for their friends’ keys, no authority above them, and its
authors call it arguably one of the first social networks; its trust is in a key, which PGP distinguishes
from trust in the key’s owner. GKK:3’s introduction is PGP’s introducer, and a friendship under
GSG is trust in the owner. Added here are politeness, the legal text with decidable conditions on it,
and what a realisation does with a clause.
Grassroots Contracts                                                                               49


Neighbouring instruments. A licence [27] presupposes an entitlement, bylaws an association, a
code of conduct and terms of membership whoever runs the forum; a grassroots contract presup-
poses none. A Ricardian contract [37] is a document an issuer signs and offers to the holders of an
instrument, readable by people and parsable by programs, whose hash every transaction record
carries; a grassroots contract has no issuer, and each participant signs an undertaking to all the
others (Section 11).

13   CONCLUSION
Proudhon’s conditions on a social contract, with two more, formalised as conditions on its acts,
give it the property he envisioned for it: any two agents may come into relation without anyone’s
leave, two instances formed independently can later become one, and every participant in an act
takes a role in it. A grassroots contract is one meeting the conditions, and its realisation has all
three.
   Such a contract has two artefacts, a legal text and a protocol realising it, and the grassroots
literature has had only the second. This paper translates the first into the second. A clause of the
text describes an act, and the acts become speech-act schemas; the schemas denote volition-guarded
transactions over a local-states function in which a state records exactly the agents its atoms name;
and three conditions on the schemas make a contract syntactically grassroots, which is enough
for the protocol realising it to be grassroots and volitionally grassroots. The semantics and the
conditions are algorithmic and the conditions are decidable, so a contract can be certified from its
text, and the only step left to judgement is the first, rendering a text as schemas. GAS’s app maker
takes a contract with its schemas, runs the checker on them, and makes a contract it certifies into
an app, shared by a link.
   Speech-act schemas have listed roles: the participants of a listed role are the agents one agent’s
state lists, as a group’s leader lists its members, so closing a group is one act of the leader and of
every member. A cleared atom deletes every atom of a state that it matches, so ending a friendship
ends every membership between the two at once.
   The same schemas determine what a realisation does with a clause, whether or not the clause
describes an act: leave it unbreachable, record its breach against the agent in breach, or record the
undertaking and who gave it. This depends on one thing: whether the act that names the agent
answerable leaves the record in another agent’s hands. Attestation and undertaking both require
that of the schemas, and differ only in whether the breach is itself an act of the contract.
   Both contracts are certified from their schemas. Who holds a record of whom is a directed graph,
and an instance is a connected component of the undirected graph of interactions. In the social
graph GSG the two coincide: acquaintance is the introductory act, a friend edge can only have
been added by the two agents it joins, and the social graph is a web of trust whose instances are
its connected components. A coin can be handed on and a message delivered to a group, so an
edge may point at an agent its source has never transacted with; an edge is added either to a
participant of the act that adds it or to an agent another participant of that act records already,
and that condition on the schemas is enough for no edge ever to join two instances. For coins it is
conservation of money.
   The conditions also leave no participant without a role. Openness lets any agent start an instance,
and without permission. Closure leaves an instance requiring no registry that issues identifiers, no
log that orders acts, and no authority that timestamps them, each of which would take part in an
act without a role in it. A community organised in this way requires no permission to form, no
authority to operate, and no proprietor to govern it.
   Three directions remain. The conditions are sufficient and not necessary, and a characterisation
would say which contracts are grassroots. Which further invariants of a contract follow from its
50                                                       James Golike, Andy Lewis-Pye, Ehud Shapiro, and Nimrod Talmon


schemas, as conservation of money does, is open. And the four things a schema carries are present,
under other names, in the contract specification languages of Section 12; defining a semantics for
one of them in which each agent holds their own state would let the conditions be checked on
contracts already written in it.
